   (Sabah 1.46 expected flips per 64 tokens vs control 1.42).
2. Add an opt-in reference-arithmetic mode in which Sabah reproduces ggml
   CUDA's Q8_1 activation quantization. That would target near-bitwise
   agreement with native CUDA by making Sabah *less* exact (§7). It is a
   compatibility mode, not a correctness fix.
3. Keep the gate and require agreement with the CPU reference instead. The
   data does not support this either: Sabah and the CPU diverge at token 38 too.

## 16. What RC4 changed

| file | change |
|---|---|
| `sabah/runtime/cuda/sabah_rt.cu` | ABI v2 with per-slot input rows; in-call eviction protection; byte verification; coverage counters; live status file; test-only sentinel under a compile-time macro |
| `patches/llama.cpp/0001-…` | ABI v2 call; CUDA graphs disabled for Sabah-routed `MUL_MAT_ID`; fusion guard |
| `patches/llama.cpp/0002-…` | new: `llama-sabah-diag` capture tool |
| `sabah/server/openai_proxy.py` | `--backend sabah|reference` with identical placement; `/health` reports live runtime counters; pinned llama.cpp build as default |
| `sabah/tools/cli.py` | `serve` requires an explicit backend choice |
| `tests/test_mul_mat_id_contract.py` | new: the ggml `MUL_MAT_ID` contract on real weights |
| `tests/test_server_contract.py` | backends differ only in the executor |
| `tools/rc4/` | the harness that produced every number in this report |



===== docs/SECOND_TIER.md (pass 1) =====

# Is a second tier worth it? (lever 5, measured)

The plan's fifth lever was to put the GTX 1080's 8 GB of VRAM in front of the
disk: on a tier miss, look in the 1080 instead of reading 635 KB from the NVMe;
on an eviction, park the victim there instead of dropping it. The projection in
the README claimed a +18% ceiling and ~54 ms/token of PCIe traffic.

That projection was about *capacity* and assumed the path was cheap. The path is
not cheap. This document is the measurement, taken with
[`tools/second_tier_probe.cu`](../tools/second_tier_probe.cu) on this rig
(RTX 5060 Ti as the serving GPU, GTX 1080 as the candidate tier, 15.38 GiB host
RAM), and the conclusion is that **no configuration of the 1080 beats the disk**,
while the one path that does beat it is host RAM - a decision this project has
already made the other way.

## What the probe measures

One expert-sized chunk (650240 B, the runtime's grain) transferred repeatedly,
per direction, alone and eight in flight, on dated hardware with the current
driver. Both GPUs are allocated, and every pair is measured, including each
device to itself (the host-RAM-tier path).

```
peer access:          0 -> 1: no        1 -> 0: no
--- via pinned host, one chunk at a time ---
  0 -> host -> 0: 0.047 ms/chunk (0.023 out + 0.024 in)  13.88 GB/s  [host-RAM tier]
  1 -> host -> 0: 0.224 ms/chunk (0.200 out + 0.024 in)   2.90 GB/s
  0 -> host -> 1: 0.230 ms/chunk (0.023 out + 0.206 in)   2.83 GB/s
  1 -> host -> 1: 0.407 ms/chunk (0.200 out + 0.207 in)   1.60 GB/s  [host-RAM tier]
--- via pinned host, 8 chunks in flight ---
  0 -> host -> 0: 0.048 ms/chunk (0.025 out + 0.024 in)  13.48 GB/s  [host-RAM tier]
  1 -> host -> 0: 0.224 ms/chunk (0.200 out + 0.024 in)   2.90 GB/s
  0 -> host -> 1: 0.231 ms/chunk (0.025 out + 0.207 in)   2.81 GB/s
  1 -> host -> 1: 0.407 ms/chunk (0.200 out + 0.207 in)   1.60 GB/s  [host-RAM tier]
--- device to device ---
  0 -> 1: no peer access (the copy falls back to the host path)
  1 -> 0: no peer access (the copy falls back to the host path)
```

Depth changes nothing: the links are already saturated at one chunk in flight
(0.047 ms at depth 1, 0.048 ms at depth 8). The link facts behind the asymmetry:
the 5060 Ti negotiates PCIe 5.0 x8, the 1080 sits at **gen3 x4** and is the
bottleneck of every path that touches it (`nvidia-smi`, and `0.200 ms` per
635 KB one way = 3.2 GB/s = gen3 x4).

For reference, this rig's disk serves a fresh 635 KB read in **~0.45 ms**
(1.4 GB/s), and the runtime's own demand path spends 0.49 ms per read.

## The economics

A second tier only pays if the traffic it *saves* costs more than the traffic it
*adds*. One expert:

| path | cost per 635 KB | vs disk |
|---|---|---|
| disk, fresh offsets | 0.45 ms | 1.0x |
| 1080 hit (1080 -> host -> 5060 Ti) | **0.224 ms** | **0.50x** |
| 1080 park (5060 Ti -> host -> 1080) | **0.230 ms** | 0.51x |
| 1080 park **plus** 1080 hit | **0.454 ms** | **1.01x** |
| host-RAM park + hit (5060 Ti -> host -> 5060 Ti) | **0.047 ms** | **0.10x** |

That middle row is the verdict. A cache entry that was ever *parked* costs
0.454 ms to serve once, and the disk would have served it in 0.45 ms. **A 1080
tier is break-even at a 100% hit rate and loses from there**, because it has no
peer access (so every transfer is a full host round trip) and its link is half
the 5060 Ti's width at a third of the generation.

Concretely, per decode token, at the measured rates (247.4 misses, 239 evictions,
121.8 ms of disk):

| policy | disk bytes served | cost of the new path | total | vs today |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| today | 247 misses | - | 121.8 ms | 1.00x |
| every miss served from the 1080, every eviction parked | 0 | 247 x 0.224 + 239 x 0.230 = 110 ms | 110 ms | 0.90x |
| the same, but only 15% of experts can be resident | 210 | 37 x 0.224 + 36 x 0.230 = 16.6 ms | 95 + 16.6 = 112 ms | 0.92x |

The first row of that table is the *impossible* case (an 8 GB tier holding the
whole working set) and it still only breaks even. The realistic case saves ~10 ms
and needs the same residency machinery.

## What is left of the idea

* **1080 as a cache: dead.** The path costs what the disk costs, and it has the
  extra failure mode of a *park* that turns a future hit into no cheaper than a
  fresh read.
* **Host RAM as a cache: real, and cheap, and off-message.** 0.047 ms per expert
  round trip, 13.9 GB/s, 9.6x cheaper than the disk. A ~6 GB pinned expert tier
  would take the working set from 9.8 GB to ~16 GB, which the measured LRU curve
  puts at ~92.5% hit, i.e. ~61 ms of disk instead of 122, plus ~145 x 0.047 =
  7 ms of second-tier traffic and ~239 parks x 0.047 = 11 ms: **~128 ms/token,
  1.5x**. But it is pinned host RAM on a machine this project exists to spare
  (the README's premise is RAM-constrained hardware, and the expert path uses
  `FILE_FLAG_NO_BUFFERING` specifically to keep host RAM free), the machine
  already carries the model's own 28.8 GB PLE mapping, and the RAM budget is the
  user's decision, not the runtime's. It is also, in effect, what the OS page
  cache would do for free if unbuffered I/O were turned off.
* **1080 as a static expert partition (compute offload): the one positive 1080
  shape.** Not caching bytes but *owning* a slice of the expert bank: ~7.5 GB
  usable holds 11.5k of the 73728 `(tensor, expert)` objects, 15.6%, so ~15% of
  lookups would never touch disk or VRAM. Ceiling: 15% of the disk time (~16 ms,
  8%) plus whatever the 1080's own compute overlaps, minus the per-layer
  activation round trips (2 x 8 KB per offloaded expert, 5.5 us, plus a
  cross-device sync per layer). It needs a second-device execution path in the
  bridge, and the win is smaller than the host-RAM tier's. Recorded as a
  candidate; not implemented.

## Reproduce

```bat
:: from a developer command prompt (vcvars64 on PATH):
nvcc -O3 -gencode arch=compute_61,code=sm_61 -gencode arch=compute_120,code=sm_120 ^
     -o second_tier_probe.exe second_tier_probe.cu
second_tier_probe.exe 8
```

The probe is self-contained: it allocates on every device it finds, measures both
directions through pinned host memory and peer-to-peer where the driver allows
it, and prints the disk comparison it should be read against.


===== docs/SUPPORTED_MODELS.md (pass 1) =====

# Supported models

## Verified

`qwen4exp` is the only architecture accepted by the Sabah accelerator today.
The validated artifact is Qwen3.8-Flash-Next UD-Q4_K_XL with contiguous,
quantization-block-aligned expert slices.

The inspector checks the GGUF metadata, tensor names, expert axis, qtype,
per-expert byte size, shard and offset. An artifact that fails these checks is
refused; Sabah does not reinterpret arbitrary MoE layouts.

## Not supported

Other GGUF architectures and any artifact with non-contiguous or misaligned
expert slices. Unsupported artifacts may be used with an external reference
runtime, but they are not accelerated by Sabah.



===== docs/V1_CORRECTNESS_CONTRACT.md (pass 1) =====

# Sabah v1.0 correctness contract (preregistration)

**Status: FROZEN.** This file, the validation corpus in `tests/v1_validation/`,
the validation driver in `tools/v1/` and the runtime sources are committed
together. That commit is `V1_CONTRACT_COMMIT`. Nothing in the validation corpus
was executed before that commit.

**After `V1_CONTRACT_COMMIT` no threshold, sample size, sampling rule, margin,
alpha, token count or pass rule in this file changes.** If the contract is found
to contain a methodological error, the attempt is declared invalid, the reason
documented, a new revision committed, and a new, untouched corpus used. Observed
validation outcomes are never used to tune a later contract.

A code change made after `V1_CONTRACT_COMMIT` (for example, to fix a defect the
validation finds) invalidates the evidence for every gate that exercised the
changed component. Those gates are rerun in full on the new implementation
commit, and evidence from different binaries is never combined into one
release.

---

## 1. What v1.0 claims, and what it does not

v1.0 means: **a reproducible, structurally exact, numerically validated
routed-MoE residency runtime with a user-accessible local API.**

It does not mean acceleration. RC4 measured Sabah at **0.2997×** stock llama.cpp
on the validation machine. Performance is measured and reported (gate L) but is
not a pass condition. No multi-GPU result exists; all multi-GPU figures remain
PROJECTED / UNVALIDATED.

## 2. Development evidence used to design this contract

Development data only, never confirmatory:

- RC4 (`results/rc4_numerical/`): one prompt, 59 prefill + 64 decode tokens.
- v1 development runs (`D:/sabah_rc4/v1dev`, prompts `dev_*`), which
  established:
  - the in-runtime self-check (max 7.99e-7 over 1,728 and 858 samples);
  - the coverage accounting rule (§6.1), confirmed exactly on a 1,010-token
    prompt split 512 + 498;
  - the capture row rule;
  - the cost of each run.

Findings that shaped the contract:

| development fact | consequence here |
|---|---|
| a wrong-expert build still produced the right greedy token at 13 of 16 steps | greedy agreement is reported, never the discriminating gate |
| the RC3 defect (wrong input row per slot) passed isolated tests | slot rows, token rows, sequences and ubatches are exercised explicitly; three sentinel classes |
| Sabah op-level error vs float64: worst 7.99e-7; llama.cpp backends 4.6e-3 – 3e-2 | op-level threshold 2e-6 |
| logit deviation from native CUDA: Sabah ≈ CPU (ratio 0.95 – 1.00); defects ≥ 4.3× | full-graph margin X = 1.25 |
| expected top-1 flips vs native CUDA ≈ 1.4 per 64 steps for both CPU and Sabah; wrong-expert sentinel 3 per 16 | flip margin δ = 0.03 |
| native CUDA Q5_1 down projection deviates ~9e-3 beyond its activation quantization, mechanism unidentified | native CUDA is a comparison anchor, never an arithmetic oracle |

## 3. Roles of the reference paths (frozen)

| role | path |
|---|---|
| structural oracle | the graph itself: router `ffn_moe_topk`, ids at `MUL_MAT_ID`, original GGUF bytes. No tolerance. |
| arithmetic oracle | float64 recomputation from gguf-py's dequantization of the original GGUF bytes and the exact captured `MUL_MAT_ID` input. In-runtime: an independent scalar host dequantizer (itself bit-identical to gguf-py, tested). |
| comparison anchor | **B = llama.cpp native CUDA**, the same graph and placement as Sabah. |
| control | **A = llama.cpp CPU** vs B: the empirical variation between two correct backends. |
| subject | **C = Sabah** vs B, judged relative to A vs B. |

The three paths differ only in `GGML_OP_OFFLOAD_MIN_BATCH` (A: 1e9; B, C: 1) and
`SABAH_LLAMA=1` for C (`tools/v1/run_path.py`). Common settings:
`-c 4096 -b 2048 -ub 512 -ngl 99 --cpu-moe -t 8 --temp 0 --seed 1`, argmax
over the full logit vector, no warm-up, hot tier 1 GiB unless stated.

## 4. Corpus (frozen, `tests/v1_validation/`)

- `prompts.jsonl`: 14 chat prompts, 2 per category: English prose, Turkish
  prose, Python code, structured JSON, math/reasoning, instruction following,
  long context (≈300–400 tokens, one ubatch). Written for v1; none was used in
  RC4 or v1 development, and none was run before this commit.
- `ubatch.jsonl`: raw token prompts of exactly **511, 512, 513 and 1100**
  tokens, the prefix of a passage generated by
  `tools/v1/make_corpus.py:ubatch_passage` (seed 20260919), tokenized by the
  pinned tokenizer.
- `manifest.json`: SHA-256 of every prompt and token array, and the fixed role
  of each prompt in each gate.

**Sample size.** 14 prompt clusters × 32 teacher-forced steps. This is bounded
by cost: with every check on, a Sabah prompt costs ~150–200 s of prefill plus
~5 s per decode step. A first plan with 64 steps and separate free-running runs
(~11–12 hours) was cut, before any validation data existed, to ~4.5–5 hours,
at the loss of power stated below. Planning assumptions, stated rather than
hidden:

- **Gate H.** With a between-prompt SD of the per-prompt error ratio of about
  0.15 (unknown; RC4 had one prompt), the SE of R at n = 14 is ≈ 0.04, so the
  one-sided 95% upper bound for a true R = 1.0 is ≈ 1.07, well below X = 1.25.
  If the true variability is much larger, the test fails. That is the
  conservative direction.
- **Gate I.** At a flip rate of ~2.2% per step, 448 steps per path give about
  10 flips each. With a design effect of 1.5 for clustering, the SE of D is
  ≈ 0.012, so the power to pass when D = 0 is ≈ 0.79. There is a ~21% chance
  that an equal-rate implementation fails gate I. This is accepted and stated.
- 14 clusters cannot detect small differences in flip propensity; the gate
  rules out large ones.

## 5. Execution plan (frozen)

Driver: `tools/v1/validate.py`, executed once, in this order, with no other
compute workload on the GPU. Windows desktop graphics clients (shell, browser,
launcher processes that WDDM lists in `nvidia-smi`) are always present on this
machine; they are recorded before every run in `attempts.jsonl` and are not
interference. "TF" = teacher-forced on B's tokens (`SABAH_DIAG_FORCE_DIR`), so all
paths are compared on identical contexts at every step.

| run | path | corpus | steps | notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 `B_free32` | B | prompts | 32, free-running | defines the forced tokens; capture as R3 |
| R2 `A_tf32` | A | prompts | 32, TF | capture as R3 |
| R3 `C_tf32` | C | prompts | 32, TF | capture `MUL_MAT_ID` in/out/ids, router top-k, weights, weighted at steps {0, 1, 16, 31}, rows rule §6.2; checks on |
| R7 `S{1,2,3}` | C with sentinel library 1, 2, 3 | sentinel role (4 prompts) | 1 (prefill), TF | capture at step 0; checks on |
| R8 `C_cache_{128M,512M,1G}` | C | cache role (2 prompts) | 8, TF | hot tier 128 MiB / 512 MiB / 1 GiB; checks on |
| R9 `C_repeat` | C | determinism role (2 prompts) | 8, TF | capture as R3; checks on |
| R11 `C_multiseq` | C | multiseq role (4 prompts) | 4, all prompts share every batch | capture at all steps with the rows rule of §6.2, which keeps **every** row of the 4-row shared decode ubatches; all 10 slots checked; checks on |
| R12 `S3_multiseq` | sentinel 3 | multiseq role | 4, shared batches | as R11 |
| R13 `C_ubatch` | C | ubatch corpus | 2 | capture at steps {0, 1}, rows rule; checks on |
| R15 API | `sabah serve --backend sabah --validate --parallel 4` | api roles | groups of 1, 2, 4 simultaneous requests, `max_tokens` 8; a stream request and its non-streamed twin, `max_tokens` 8 | |
| R16 API A/B | `sabah serve --backend reference` vs `--backend sabah` | `api_ab` role | sequential, `max_tokens` 16 | reported |
| R17 benchmark | stock llama.cpp vs Sabah, checks off | benchmark role (1 prompt) | 64, free-running | order stock, Sabah, stock, Sabah; after R18 has finished |
| R18 build | pristine `96ffdc41c` + patches 0001/0002 | — | — | clean CUDA build + smoke; runs in parallel with R1–R16 |

A, B and C carry **identical instrumentation** in R1–R3, so the full-graph
comparison of gates H and I is between identically observed graphs. Observing
a tensor splits the graph there and disables CUDA fusion across it, for B and C
alike.

"Checks on" means `SABAH_LLAMA_SELFCHECK=2` and
`SABAH_LLAMA_VERIFY_BYTES=fetch`. The self-check compares two hash-selected
(token, slot) outputs per expert op against float64 at 2e-6. Byte
verification covers every fetched expert slice plus every 64th hit, selected
by the hit counter.

## 6. Hard gates

### 6.1 Gate B — structural exactness (every Sabah run)

- **Coverage exact.** For each ubatch with `n` rows and `o` output rows:
  - `o = 0`: 141 calls and `141·n` token-rows;
  - `o > 0`: 144 calls and `141·n + 3·o` token-rows.

  The run totals (runtime counters) must equal the sum over the run's ubatches,
  computed from prompt lengths, `-ub 512` and steps. For the API (§6.8) the
  per-group rule is `Δtokens = 141·ΣP + 3·N + 144·ΣD`. Any mismatch means an
  expert op did not reach Sabah (silent fallback) or ran with wrong rows:
  **FAIL**.
- `lookups = 10 × tokens`, `empty_calls = 0`.
- `verify_fail = 0` wherever verification is on, and `verify_ok > 0`.
- On every captured row: ids at `MUL_MAT_ID` equal the router's
  `ffn_moe_topk` (0 mismatches), and `ffn_moe_weighted` equals `ffn_moe_down ×
  weight` of the same slot, bitwise (0 mismatches).
- The shipped library reports `sentinel=0` in every product run.

### 6.2 Gate C — operation-level float64 accuracy

- **Rows.** For every captured tensor with token extent `n`, keep rows
  {0, ⌊n/3⌋, ⌊2n/3⌋, n−1} (all rows if n ≤ 4, so the 4-row shared decode
  ubatches of R11/R12 are checked in full).
- **Slots.** For every kept row of every captured ubatch, every block and every
  family f ∈ {gate, up, down}:

      c     = int(sha256("prompt|step|ubatch|row|f")[:16], 16) mod 10
      slots = {(block + c) mod 10, (block + c + 5) mod 10}    (R11/R12: all 10)

- **Check.** Each selected output is recomputed in float64 from the captured
  input row `slot mod ne11` and the captured id (`tools/v1/opcheck_v1.py`).

PASS iff, for every prompt of R3:

- every sample has **relative L2 ≤ 2e-6**;
- each captured step has all 48 blocks × 3 families;
- each family covers all 10 slots;
- n_samples > 0.

In addition, the in-runtime self-check reports `selfcheck_fail = 0` in every
product run.

### 6.3 Gate D — wrong-expert discrimination

Test-only libraries built with `-DSABAH_SENTINEL=k`; the shipped library
never contains this code path:

- 1: slot 0 executes the neighbouring expert;
- 2: expert slot i reads input row (i+1) mod n_used;
- 3: token t reads the input row of token (t+1) mod n_tokens.

PASS iff **all 12 trials** of R7 (3 libraries × 4 prompts, prefill step) are detected by
**both** detectors: the gate-C rule (at least one sample > 2e-6) and the
in-runtime self-check (`selfcheck_fail > 0`), **and** R12 is detected by the
gate-C rule.

Greedy-token agreement of the sentinel runs is reported, to document that
the gate is stronger than greedy identity.

### 6.4 Gate E — residency invariance and determinism

PASS iff:

- logits of R8 are **bitwise identical** across 128 MiB, 512 MiB and 1 GiB,
  for both prompts and all 8 steps, with `verify_fail = 0` and
  `selfcheck_fail = 0`;
- R9's logits are bitwise identical to the first 8 steps of the same prompts
  in R3 (same instrumentation).

### 6.5 Gate F — multi-sequence and concurrency

- **F1 (R11).** Gates B and C pass with all rows and all slots checked, and
  every shared-batch decode step carries 4 rows from 4 sequences. R12 must be
  detected (gate D).
- **F2 (R15).** For every request group of 1, 2 and 4 simultaneous requests:
  - every response is 200 and complete;
  - accounting is exact (§6.1);
  - Δ`selfcheck_ok > 0`, Δ`selfcheck_fail = 0`, Δ`verify_fail = 0`;
  - no server crash.

### 6.6 Gate G — prefill longer than an ubatch

R13 (511, 512, 513, 1100 tokens) PASS iff:

- rc = 0;
- coverage exact (1100 tokens = ubatches of 512, 512, 76, only the last with
  an output);
- self-check and verification clean;
- gate C holds on every captured row;
- **every ubatch** of each prefill is represented in the captures for blocks
  0–46, and block 47 in the final ubatch.

### 6.7 Gates H and I — statistical, on R1 (B), R2 (A), R3 (C)

For prompt p and step s ∈ {0..31}:

- e_P(p,s) = ‖L_P − L_B‖₂ / ‖L_B‖₂ over the full logit vector;
- flip_P(p,s) = [argmax L_P ≠ argmax L_B].

Contexts are identical by construction.

**H — full-graph non-inferiority.**
m_P(p) = mean_s e_P(p,s), and **R = Σ_p m_C(p) / Σ_p m_A(p)**, a ratio of sums,
so no per-prompt denominator can be near zero.

**I — greedy flip non-inferiority.**
**D = (Σ flip_C − Σ flip_A) / (14 · 32)**. All flips count, near ties included;
the reference margins at flips are reported.

**Bootstrap.** Paired cluster bootstrap over prompts: 20,000 resamples of the
14 prompt ids with replacement, with the same resample applied to A and C,
`numpy.random.default_rng(20260919)`. The upper bound is the 95th percentile
of the statistic (one-sided 95%; alpha = 0.05).

- **Gate H PASS iff UB(R) ≤ 1.25.**
- **Gate I PASS iff UB(D) ≤ 0.03.**

Raw observation counts (14 × 32 = 448 per path) and the cluster count (14) are
both reported. Observations within a prompt are correlated; only prompts are
resampled.

Free-running greedy statistics are reported per prompt, not gated: first
divergence, and exact agreement at 16 and at 32 tokens. They are derived
exactly from R2/R3: until the first disagreement a free-running run and a run
teacher-forced on B's tokens have identical contexts, so the first free-running
divergence is the first step whose argmax differs from B's. A 64-token
free-running comparison is not run for v1 (RC4 reported one).

### 6.8 Gates J and K — API and evidence-grade counters

- **J.** `/health` of the R15 server reports `backend = llama.cpp+sabah` and
  `execution = SABAH_NATIVE_MUL_MAT_ID`. F2 passes. The streamed request
  returns more than one chunk, and its concatenated text equals the
  non-streamed twin's text exactly. Its counters are exact.
- **K.** Every counter check in gates B, F2 and J is exact. Counters come from
  `sabah_rt`'s status file, which is written under the residency lock after
  every call.

### 6.9 Gate A — reproducibility

The patches apply to a pristine `96ffdc41c` and reproduce the tested sources
byte for byte (modulo line endings). A clean CUDA build of that tree completes,
and its `llama-sabah-diag` produces the same argmax for development prompt
`dev_rc4` as the tested build.

### 6.10 Gate M — claim hygiene (checklist)

- The README states the measured throughput ratio and says the configuration
  is slower than stock llama.cpp, if it is.
- It has a "What v1.0 means" section.
- The multi-GPU figures are labelled PROJECTED / UNVALIDATED.
- No claim of bit-exact end-to-end execution.
- No sentence claims measured acceleration on the validation machine.

### 6.11 Gate L — benchmark (value, not pass/fail)

R17 reports prefill and decode tok/s, per-step latency, hit rate, bytes fetched
and `MEASURED_SPEEDUP = Sabah / stock` (decode). AVAILABLE iff all four runs
complete.

## 7. Crash, missing data and reruns

- A non-zero exit, illegal memory access, CUDA error, missing output file,
  missing capture record or failed request is a **FAIL of every gate that
  depends on that run**. Nothing is averaged away.
- A run may be repeated **once**, only if it was invalidated by documented
  external interference (another GPU process, sleep/power event, disk full, OS
  termination). The reason is recorded in the evidence. A run is never repeated
  because its result failed a gate.
- The only repetition for determinism is R9, as preregistered.

## 8. Environment

The validation machine: RTX 4050 Laptop 6 GB (sm_89), ~30 GB RAM, Windows 11,
CUDA 13.3, llama.cpp `96ffdc41c` + patches 0001/0002, model
`Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-UD-Q4_K_XL` (shard 1 SHA-256 `44481862…3ec8082`). The
runtime DLL and llama.cpp binaries are built from `V1_CONTRACT_COMMIT`, and
their SHA-256 values are recorded in `results/v1_validation/environment.json`.

## 9. Release rule

`v1.0.0` is created only if gates A–K and M pass and L is AVAILABLE. Otherwise
the next RC is recorded with, for each failed gate: the observed value, the
rule above, the root cause if known, whether code changed, and what evidence
remains valid.


===== docs/V1_RELEASE_REPORT.md (pass 1) =====

# Sabah Accelerator v1 release report

## Release verdict

```text
v1.0.0 NOT READY
Release candidate produced instead: v0.9.0-rc4 (see RC4_NUMERICAL_EQUIVALENCE_REPORT.md)
```

The exact expert/block runtime is real and tested. Gate F's first real
full-model Sabah token now passes through the reproducible native
`MUL_MAT_ID` boundary, but the multi-token numerical ladder shows measured
CPU-reference versus CUDA expert arithmetic differences, so shipping `v1.0.0`
would overstate the result. Detailed RC3 evidence is in
[`RC3_CORRECTNESS_REPORT.md`](RC3_CORRECTNESS_REPORT.md).

The llama.cpp archaeology and implemented native seam are recorded in
[`LLAMA_CPP_INTEGRATION_MAP.md`](LLAMA_CPP_INTEGRATION_MAP.md). The selected
seam is the existing `GGML_OP_MUL_MAT_ID` backend/scheduler boundary. Sabah
now receives the real host GGUF tensor, authoritative IDs and device hidden
states there, uses a native LRU and exact quantized expert execution, then
returns to the normal graph.

Starting repository commit: `1bc838c67d11c48a8740306ea4a036dea965ddf9`.

RC3 package: `dist/sabah_moe_accelerator-0.9.0rc3-py3-none-any.whl`.
SHA-256: `6d54780040ee9a8dff78ffb19da72e5a51f87dc99d9de5e56b14d2f28366e611`.

## Gates

| Gate | Result | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| A — build | PASS | CUDA 13.3 + MSVC build of `sabah_rt.dll` and `mgpu_qualify.exe` |
| B — real model inspection | PASS | Qwen3.8-Flash-Next UD-Q4_K_XL: 48 blocks, 512 experts, 4 shards, 111.335 GB; layout verified |
| C — real expert runtime | PASS | Real GGUF expert bytes loaded and executed on RTX 4050 |
| D — hot-tier cache | PASS | Hit/miss, LRU eviction, reload and residency independence exercised |
| E — expert/MoE correctness | PASS | Four qtypes `max|diff|=0`; block relative L2 `6.03e-7` / `6.02e-7`; wrong-expert discrimination PASS |
| F — end-to-end model | FIRST TOKEN PASS / MULTI-TOKEN BLOCKED | Clean native bridge reproduces the first token; all-block multi-token comparison exposes CPU-reference vs CUDA expert arithmetic drift |
| G — server | PARTIAL | OpenAI-compatible localhost proxy works in explicitly labelled reference mode |
| H — user flow | PARTIAL | inspect/qualify/plan/selftest/benchmark/serve reference flow documented |
| I — claim hygiene | PASS | measured/projected/speedup-unavailable states are separated |

## Current-machine measurements

Machine qualification on 2026-09-18:

- RTX 4050 Laptop GPU, 6.44 GB nominal / 4.75 GB usable
- 29.8 GB RAM / 25.5 GB usable
- simultaneous H2D: 12.73 GB/s; one-device contention factor: 1.00
- storage read at model path: 2.43 GB/s

Real block A/B on block 0, 64 measured tokens, real routing trace:

| Hot tier | Hit rate | ms/token | GPU wait | Host stage | Fetch |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 16 slots | 0.0797 | 11.804 | 0.243 ms | 7.981 ms | 28.27 MB/token |
| 64 slots | 0.2766 | 5.126 | 0.197 ms | 2.946 ms | 22.22 MB/token |
| 512 slots | 1.0000 | 1.312 | 0.000 ms | 0.000 ms | 0 MB/token |

The 512-slot row is the one-block kernel cost. It is not full-model
tokens/second. The 16-slot streaming penalty against that row was measured at
10.492 ms/token.

## Correctness

The CUDA self-test used real expert bytes from blocks 0 and 2:

- Q4_K, Q5_1, Q5_K, and Q8_0 decode matched gguf-py with zero reported max
  absolute difference.
- GPU block output relative L2 was `6.030e-7` for block 0 and `6.015e-7`
  for block 2.
- Replacing one routed expert changed output by `4.680e-1` and `6.657e-1`,
  proving the test detects a wrong expert.
- Forced eviction/refetch returned bit-identical output.

The first-token result is recorded in
[`results/full_model_first_token.json`](../results/full_model_first_token.json):
reference and Sabah both sampled token ID `1596` (`We`). The result also
records native cache counters. Router-weight, intermediate-state, MoE
aggregate and logit error dumps are explicitly marked unavailable rather than
being inferred from final text.

## Projection and unvalidated work

No end-to-end Sabah speedup is claimed. Multi-GPU H2D, sharding, and any
3×3060 result remain `UNVALIDATED`. Existing planner values are projections
from measured single-device inputs and historical traces.

The same target GGUF was also loaded and run for a one-token deterministic
reference smoke test through llama.cpp; its SHA-256 is recorded in
`results/reference_baseline_single_token.json`. That run is a validity smoke
test, not a representative throughput baseline, and no Sabah speedup is
derived from it.

## Known limitations

See [`KNOWN_LIMITATIONS.md`](KNOWN_LIMITATIONS.md). In particular, the current
30 GB-class machine cannot hold the 77.018 GB expert bank in RAM, so the
storage-backed path is primarily for correctness and runtime validation.

## Next work

1. Establish a numerically equivalent reference backend (or a documented
   semantic tolerance) and close the all-block router/intermediate/logit ladder.
2. Replace the reference-mode API backend with the integrated Sabah backend.
3. Re-run identical full-model reference/Sabah benchmarks before considering
   `v1.0.0`.

# Sabah v1.0 — validation report

**Did the preregistered v1.0 contract pass? Yes, on the second API attempt,
as the contract prescribes.** Attempt 1 failed three API gates on a real proxy
defect. The defect was fixed, the affected runs were rerun in full, and every
gate passed. Both attempts are recorded here and in
`results/v1_validation/`.

| identity | value |
|---|---|
| `V1_CONTRACT_COMMIT` | `ca0a222777d1bff1461d079826b0043a67848b5d` (`docs/V1_CORRECTNESS_CONTRACT.md`) |
| implementation commit, diag runs R1–R13, R17 | `ca0a222` |
| implementation commit, API runs R15–R16 | `3395267` (proxy timeout fix; see §3) |
| llama.cpp | `96ffdc41ceb055e1c2d3d96667ae6d9f0ccb710b` + patches 0001/0002 (hashes in `environment.json`) |
| model | Qwen3.8-Flash-Next UD-Q4_K_XL, shard 1 SHA-256 `44481862…3ec8082` |

| runtime / llama.cpp binaries | byte-identical across both attempts (`environment*.json`) |
| tests | 23 (unit, server contract, native `MUL_MAT_ID` contract incl. 3 sentinels) |
| corpus | 14 chat prompts (7 categories × 2) + 4 boundary prompts (511, 512, 513, 1100 tokens); never executed before the contract commit |
| decode steps | 32 teacher-forced steps per prompt per path (448 per path) |

## 2. Numbers

**Operation level.** Each sampled `MUL_MAT_ID` output was recomputed in
float64 from its own captured input and ids against gguf-py's dequantization
of the original GGUF bytes. 27,972 samples, worst relative L2 9.25e-7. The
in-runtime self-check, an independent host dequantizer, found no failure
across all product runs.

**Full graph (H).** Per-prompt ratio of mean logit error vs native CUDA
(Sabah / CPU), from 0.80 (`turkish_prose_1`) to 1.25 (`python_code_1`). The
aggregate is R = 0.981 (paired prompt-cluster bootstrap, 20,000 resamples, seed
20260919, one-sided 95% UB 1.033).

**Greedy (I).** Teacher-forced flips vs native CUDA: CPU 3, Sabah 4, of 448
steps each. Reference margins at Sabah's flips: 0.018, 0.065, 0.129 and
**1.348** (`json_2`, step 2 — not a near tie). CPU's flips: 0.065, 0.129,
0.129. Free-running agreement, derived exactly from the same runs: identical
for 16 tokens on 12/14 prompts (CPU 13/14), and for 32 tokens on 10/14 (CPU
11/14).

**API A/B.** Reference vs Sabah through `sabah serve`, 4 prompts × 16 tokens:
**4/4 identical**.

**Performance (MEASURED).** Prompt `math_1`, 64 tokens, 2 runs per path,
checks off:

| path | decode tok/s | median ms/token | prefill tok/s |
|---|---|---|---|
| stock llama.cpp | 1.624 / 1.615 | 517 / 536 | 0.82 |
| Sabah | 0.410 / 0.412 | 2,248 / 2,330 | 0.68 |

**MEASURED SPEEDUP = 0.254**: Sabah is about 3.9× slower on this machine.
Sabah's hot-tier hit rate was 46%, and 122.5 GB moved over PCIe. The machine
cannot hold the 77 GB expert bank in RAM; see `KNOWN_LIMITATIONS.md`.

## 3. Attempt 1: what failed and what was done

In attempt 1, the API runs failed gates F2, J and K. Under `--validate`, the
4-request group took longer than the proxy's hard-coded **600 s** backend
timeout. The proxy returned HTTP 502 for all four requests while llama-server
kept computing, and that work then landed in the next request's counter
window. The exact counters caught it: expected 7,920 token-rows, observed
8,276.

- **Root cause:** a fixed timeout in `sabah/server/openai_proxy.py`.
- **Fix (`3395267`):** no backend read limit by default, with an opt-in
  `--backend-timeout`, plus regression tests using a deliberately slow
  backend.
- **Scope:** only R15 and R16 load the proxy. Their attempt-1 outputs are
  archived (`attempt1_api_failure.json`), and both were rerun in full on
  `3395267`. The runtime and llama.cpp binaries are byte-identical across the
  attempts, so all other evidence remains valid under contract §0.
- **Rules:** no threshold, sample or rule was changed.

## 4. Development evidence vs confirmatory evidence

RC4 (`results/rc4_numerical/`) and the v1 development runs (`dev_*` prompts)
designed the contract. Only the frozen corpus above is confirmatory.

## 5. Known limitations

See `KNOWN_LIMITATIONS.md`, in particular:

- the tested configuration is slower than stock llama.cpp;
- one machine, one model, 14 prompt clusters;
- gate I has ~0.79 power at its margin;
- full-graph comparisons were made on identically instrumented graphs;
- llama.cpp's native CUDA Q5_1 down-projection residual is unexplained.

Multi-GPU remains PROJECTED / UNVALIDATED.


===== sabah/runtime/cuda/sabah_rt.cu (pass 1) =====

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Sabah runtime - CUDA side.
//
// This file is the part of Sabah that is allowed to touch the model's
// arithmetic, and therefore the part that must not change it. If the router
// selects expert E, these kernels execute expert E's real quantized weights.
// There is no approximation, no substitution and no predicted stand-in.
//
// What it provides:
//   * device allocation for expert slots and activations
//   * pinned host staging + an async H2D copy stream with events
//   * dequantize-and-matvec kernels for the quant types the Flash-Next
//     expert bank actually uses: Q4_K, Q5_K (gate/up) and Q5_1, Q8_0 (down)
//   * a standalone dequantizer, so the kernels above can be checked against
//     gguf-py's reference implementation instead of being trusted
//
// Quant layouts follow ggml-common.h exactly; the block geometries are
// asserted against the model at load time on the Python side.
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <cuda_fp16.h>
#if defined(_WIN32)
  #ifndef NOMINMAX
  #define NOMINMAX
  #endif
  #include <windows.h>
  #include <psapi.h>
  #pragma comment(lib, "psapi.lib")
#else
  #include <fcntl.h>
  #include <unistd.h>
  #include <sys/stat.h>
#endif
#include <algorithm>
#include <chrono>
#include <cstdlib>
#include <mutex>
#include <stdio.h>
#include <string.h>
#include <cstring>
#include <cstdio>
#include <cmath>
#include <map>
#include <vector>
#include <condition_variable>
#include <deque>
#include <thread>
#include <utility>

// The IQ-family expert quants need ggml's grid/sign lookup tables. They are
// extracted verbatim from ggml-common.h by tools/gen_iq_tables.py; plain
// `static const`, so both the host reference dequantizer and the device
// sub-block kernels can read the same arrays (exactly as ggml does).
#include "sabah_iq_tables.h"

#if defined(_WIN32)
  #define SABAH_API __declspec(dllexport)
#else
  #define SABAH_API __attribute__((visibility("default")))
#endif

// ---- quant type codes (ggml enum values, so traces stay comparable) --------
// The four the UD-Q4_K_XL artifact uses...
#define SB_Q5_1      7
#define SB_Q8_0      8
#define SB_Q4_K     12
#define SB_Q5_K     13
// ...and the six the GSQ-RCO IQ3_S artifact uses.
#define SB_IQ3_XXS  18
#define SB_IQ4_NL   20
#define SB_IQ3_S    21
#define SB_IQ2_S    22
#define SB_IQ4_XS   23
#define SB_Q2_0     42

// Test-only sentinel builds (never defined for a shipped library):
//   1  slot 0 of every call executes the neighbouring expert
//   2  expert slot i reads input row (i+1) mod n_used   (the RC3 bug class)
//   3  token t reads input row of token (t+1) mod n_tokens  (cross-sequence)
#ifndef SABAH_SENTINEL
#define SABAH_SENTINEL 0
#endif
#ifdef SABAH_SENTINEL_WRONG_EXPERT
#undef SABAH_SENTINEL
#define SABAH_SENTINEL 1
#endif

static char g_err[512] = {0};
static cudaStream_t g_copy    = 0;
static cudaStream_t g_compute = 0;

// The Python block executor and the llama.cpp adapter share this native
// residency core.  Entries are keyed by the logical source tensor pointer and
// expert id; the source tensor pointer is stable for the lifetime of a loaded
// GGUF model.  A single global byte budget is used so gate/up/down tensors
// compete for the same hot tier instead of creating three independent caches.
struct sb_mmid_entry {
    const void * source_key;
    int expert;
    void * device;
    size_t bytes;
    uint64_t touch;
    uint64_t epoch;     // last MUL_MAT_ID call that referenced this slot
    bool slab;          // true: came from the hot-tier slab; false: over-budget
    bool filled;        // true: the expert bytes are already in `device`
};

// Per-phase cost of a MUL_MAT_ID call, in milliseconds, accumulated over the run.
// Reported via SABAH_LLAMA_TRACE so tuning is driven by measurement rather than
// by which part of the path looks expensive.
static double g_t_slot  = 0;   // residency lookup + allocation + eviction
static double g_t_fetch = 0;   // resolve + disk reads + host-to-device copies
static double g_t_wait  = 0;   // of which: blocked on the reads
static double g_t_ids   = 0;   // router id download and its stream synchronise
static double g_t_ptr   = 0;   // pointer table upload and copy/compute hand-off
static double g_t_prime = 0;   // residency snapshot: one table sweep per tensor

// Optional CUDA-event probe (SABAH_LLAMA_EVENTS=1). Attributes a slowdown to the
// kernels themselves (clock/width) or to the gaps around them (host submission,
// queue depth) without nsys, which cannot intercept this DLL. Every k_mul_mat_id
// launch is bracketed by an event pair; the pairs are read at the next router-id
// drain, which already guarantees they have completed. Reported per token.
struct sb_ev_pair {
    cudaEvent_t a, b;
    double host_ms;
    int nt;
    int rows;
};
static bool                 g_ev_enabled = false;
static bool                 g_ev_checked = false;
static double               g_ev_kernel_ms = 0;   // device time inside the kernels (decode rows only)
static double               g_ev_launch_ms = 0;   // host time to submit each launch (decode rows only)
static uint64_t             g_ev_calls = 0;
static double               g_ev_kernel_ms_all = 0; // every call, for reference
static double               g_ev_launch_ms_all = 0;
static uint64_t             g_ev_calls_all = 0;
static double               g_ev_kernel_ms_gu = 0;  // decode gate/up calls (rows < 1024)
static uint64_t             g_ev_calls_gu = 0;
static double               g_ev_kernel_ms_dn = 0;  // decode down calls (rows >= 1024)
static uint64_t             g_ev_calls_dn = 0;
static std::vector<sb_ev_pair> g_ev_pending;
static std::vector<sb_ev_pair> g_ev_pool;

static bool sb_ev_on() {
    if (!g_ev_checked) {
        g_ev_checked = true;
        const char * v = std::getenv("SABAH_LLAMA_EVENTS");
        g_ev_enabled = v != nullptr && v[0] != 0 && v[0] != '0';
    }
    return g_ev_enabled;
}

// Optional 4-rows-per-block k_mul_mat_id tile (SABAH_LLAMA_TILE2=1). It is a
// throughput change only: the floating-point reduction order is reproduced
// exactly, so the greedy token stream must not move by one bit.
static bool                 g_tile2_enabled = false;
static bool                 g_tile2_checked = false;

static bool sb_tile2_on() {
    if (!g_tile2_checked) {
        g_tile2_checked = true;
        const char * v = std::getenv("SABAH_LLAMA_TILE2");
        g_tile2_enabled = v != nullptr && v[0] != 0 && v[0] != '0';
    }
    return g_tile2_enabled;
}

// TILE3 shape constants. SB_T3_V is the widest reduction the staged tree can
// hold (three virtual warps); anything wider falls back to k_mul_mat_id.
#define SB_T3_ROWS 16
#define SB_T3_LANES 8
#define SB_T3_V 96

static bool                 g_tile3_enabled = false;
static bool                 g_tile3_checked = false;
static int                  g_tile3_maxnsub = -1;

// On by default. Byte-identical output against the reference reduction is
// proven by tools/tile_exact.cu (260,000 rows, raw bits) and by whole-file
// logits comparisons (REPORT.md 8). SABAH_LLAMA_TILE3=0 is the escape hatch to
// the original kernel; SABAH_LLAMA_TILE3_MAXNSUB exists so the fallback branch
// can be exercised on a model that is otherwise inside the guard.
static bool sb_tile3_on() {
    if (!g_tile3_checked) {
        g_tile3_checked = true;
        const char * v = std::getenv("SABAH_LLAMA_TILE3");
        g_tile3_enabled = true;
        if (v != nullptr && (v[0] == '0' || v[0] == 'n' || v[0] == 'N' || v[0] == 'f' || v[0] == 'F')) {
            g_tile3_enabled = false;
        }
    }
    return g_tile3_enabled;
}

static int sb_tile3_maxnsub() {
    if (g_tile3_maxnsub < 0) {
        g_tile3_maxnsub = SB_T3_V;
        if (const char * v = std::getenv("SABAH_LLAMA_TILE3_MAXNSUB")) {
            const int n = std::atoi(v);
            if (n > 0) {
                g_tile3_maxnsub = n;
            }
        }
    }
    return g_tile3_maxnsub;
}

struct sb_phase_timer {
    std::chrono::steady_clock::time_point t0;
    double * acc;
    explicit sb_phase_timer(double * a) : t0(std::chrono::steady_clock::now()), acc(a) {}
    ~sb_phase_timer() {
        *acc += std::chrono::duration<double, std::milli>(
                    std::chrono::steady_clock::now() - t0).count();
    }
};

static std::mutex g_mmid_mutex;
static std::vector<sb_mmid_entry> g_mmid_entries;
static size_t g_mmid_capacity = 0;
static size_t g_mmid_bytes = 0;
static uint64_t g_mmid_tick = 0;
static uint64_t g_mmid_hits = 0;
static uint64_t g_mmid_misses = 0;
static uint64_t g_mmid_evictions = 0;
static uint64_t g_mmid_bytes_fetched = 0;
// Slots reserved on behalf of the tensor that follows this call in the same
// layer, so that a layer's three tensors share one disk wave (merged fetch).
static uint64_t g_mp_reserved = 0;
// Diagnostics. `epoch` identifies the MUL_MAT_ID call in progress: a slot whose
// pointer has already been handed to that call must not be evicted before the
// call's kernel has run, whatever the capacity says.
static uint64_t g_mmid_epoch = 0;
// The router ids of one layer's three expert tensors are the same tensor, so the
// downloads after the first of a group are redundant. Reuse is allowed only
// while the ids tensor is unchanged and the group stays this short; that bound
// is also the width of the eviction-protection window above.
static constexpr uint64_t SB_ID_GROUP_MAX = 4;
static uint64_t g_mmid_calls = 0;
static uint64_t g_mmid_tokens = 0;
static uint64_t g_id_reuse = 0;       // calls that reused the previous call's ids
static uint64_t g_id_group_max = 0;   // longest reuse streak observed
static bool     g_id_check = false;   // SABAH_LLAMA_ID_CHECK=1: prove the reuse
static uint64_t g_id_check_ok = 0, g_id_check_fail = 0;
static uint64_t g_mmid_overflow = 0;   // allocations that exceeded capacity
static uint64_t g_mmid_verify_ok = 0;
static uint64_t g_mmid_verify_fail = 0;
static bool     g_mmid_verify = false;     // SABAH_LLAMA_VERIFY_BYTES=1 | fetch
static bool     g_mmid_verify_all_hits = false;
static uint64_t g_mmid_verify_hit_every = 64; // "fetch" mode: every Nth hit, by counter
static uint64_t g_mmid_verify_budget = UINT64_MAX; // how many checks may touch the mapped oracle
static uint64_t g_mmid_empty_calls = 0;    // MUL_MAT_ID with zero token rows (no-op)
// in-runtime float64 self-check (SABAH_LLAMA_SELFCHECK=K samples per call)
static int      g_sc_k = 0;
static double   g_sc_tol = 2e-6;
static uint64_t g_sc_ok = 0;
static uint64_t g_sc_fail = 0;
static double   g_sc_max = 0.0;
static uint64_t g_sc_slot[16] = {0};
static uint64_t g_sc_multi_token = 0;      // samples taken from calls with >1 token row
static std::vector<const void *> g_sc_tensors;   // distinct expert tensors checked

static bool ck(cudaError_t e, const char * what);

// Read a byte count written as a plain integer, in scientific notation, or with
// a binary multiplier suffix ("9800000000", "9.8e9", "9.8G", "9800MiB").
//
// This used to be a plain strtoull(..., 10), and strtoull stops at the first
// character it does not understand. The documented value "9.8e9" therefore
// parsed as 9 - nine bytes - and because 9 bytes is a valid (if useless) answer
// nothing complained. The hot tier then failed to hold even one expert, every
// miss fell through to a fresh cudaMalloc, and decode ran 4x slower than the
// documented figure with no error anywhere. A value that cannot be read whole
// is now refused, and a tier too small to hold more than a few experts is said
// out loud.
static bool sb_parse_size(const char * text, size_t & out) {
    if (text == nullptr || *text == '\0') return false;
    char * end = nullptr;
    const double value = std::strtod(text, &end);
    if (end == text || !(value > 0)) return false;

    double multiplier = 1.0;
    if (*end != '\0') {
        switch (*end) {
            case 'k': case 'K': multiplier = 1024.0;                       break;
            case 'm': case 'M': multiplier = 1024.0 * 1024.0;              break;
            case 'g': case 'G': multiplier = 1024.0 * 1024.0 * 1024.0;     break;
            case 't': case 'T': multiplier = 1024.0 * 1024.0 * 1024.0 * 1024.0; break;
            default: return false;
        }
        const char * rest = end + 1;
        if (*rest == 'i' || *rest == 'I') ++rest;
        if (*rest == 'b' || *rest == 'B') ++rest;
        if (*rest != '\0') return false;
    }

    const double bytes = value * multiplier;
    if (!(bytes > 0) || bytes > 1.0e15) return false;
    out = (size_t) bytes;
    return true;
}

// Below this a hot tier holds a handful of experts, which is never what anyone
// meant: it is the shape of a value that did not parse the way it was written.
static const size_t SB_TIER_SUSPICIOUSLY_SMALL = (size_t) 256 * 1024 * 1024;

static size_t sb_mmid_capacity_bytes() {
    const char * env = std::getenv("SABAH_LLAMA_HOT_BYTES");
    if (env && *env) {
        size_t value = 0;
        if (sb_parse_size(env, value)) {
            if (value < SB_TIER_SUSPICIOUSLY_SMALL) {
                std::fprintf(stderr,
                             "SABAH_LLAMA: SABAH_LLAMA_HOT_BYTES=%s is only %llu bytes. "
                             "A tier that small holds almost nothing, so every expert miss "
                             "ends up as a fresh cudaMalloc and decode gets much slower. "
                             "Did you mean %s000000000?\n",
                             env, (unsigned long long) value, env);
                std::fflush(stderr);
            }
            return value;
        }
        std::fprintf(stderr,
                     "SABAH_LLAMA: cannot read SABAH_LLAMA_HOT_BYTES=%s. Use a plain byte "
                     "count or a binary suffix (9.8G, 9800M); using the default 2 GiB.\n",
                     env);
        std::fflush(stderr);
    }
    return (size_t) 2ull * 1024ull * 1024ull * 1024ull;
}

// ---------------- Device slab allocator -------------------------------------
//
// One cudaMalloc for the whole hot tier, subdivided on demand. The point is to
// remove cudaMalloc/cudaFree from the per-miss path: a miss turns into a
// best-fit search over a free list and a pointer offset, not an allocator call
// (and, more importantly, no free/malloc churn that can serialise the device).
//
// Every allocation is a sub-range [base+offset, base+offset+bytes). `free`
// returns the range and coalesces it with any adjacent free range, so the pool
// does not fragment under the miss/evict steady state.
static void * g_slab_base = nullptr;
static size_t g_slab_size = 0;
static std::vector<std::pair<size_t, size_t>> g_slab_free;   // (offset, bytes)
static std::map<void *, size_t> g_slab_alloc;                // ptr -> bytes
static std::mutex g_slab_mutex;

static void sb_slab_destroy() {
    std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(g_slab_mutex);
    if (g_slab_base) {
        cudaFree(g_slab_base);
        g_slab_base = nullptr;
    }
    g_slab_size = 0;
    g_slab_free.clear();
    g_slab_alloc.clear();
}

static void sb_slab_init(size_t capacity_bytes) {
    std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(g_slab_mutex);
    if (g_slab_base) return;
    void * p = nullptr;
    if (!ck(cudaMalloc(&p, capacity_bytes), "sabah slab alloc")) return;
    g_slab_base = p;
    g_slab_size = capacity_bytes;
    g_slab_free.clear();
    g_slab_free.push_back({ 0, capacity_bytes });
    g_slab_alloc.clear();
}

static void * sb_slab_alloc(size_t bytes) {
    std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(g_slab_mutex);
    if (!g_slab_base) {
        // lazy init without re-locking
        size_t cap = sb_mmid_capacity_bytes();
        void * p = nullptr;
        if (!ck(cudaMalloc(&p, cap), "sabah slab alloc")) return nullptr;
        g_slab_base = p;
        g_slab_size = cap;
        g_slab_free.clear();
        g_slab_free.push_back({ 0, cap });
        g_slab_alloc.clear();
    }
    // best fit: smallest free range that still holds the request
    size_t best_i = g_slab_free.size(), best_sz = 0;
    for (size_t i = 0; i < g_slab_free.size(); ++i) {
        const size_t sz = g_slab_free[i].second;
        if (sz >= bytes && (best_i == g_slab_free.size() || sz < best_sz)) {
            best_i = i; best_sz = sz;
        }
    }
    if (best_i == g_slab_free.size()) return nullptr;   // pool exhausted
    const size_t off = g_slab_free[best_i].first;
    if (best_sz == bytes) {
        g_slab_free.erase(g_slab_free.begin() + (long) best_i);
    } else {
        g_slab_free[best_i] = { off + bytes, best_sz - bytes };
    }
    void * ptr = (char *) g_slab_base + off;
    g_slab_alloc[ptr] = bytes;
    return ptr;
}

static void sb_slab_free(void * ptr) {
    if (!ptr) return;
    std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(g_slab_mutex);
    if (!g_slab_base) return;
    auto it = g_slab_alloc.find(ptr);
    if (it == g_slab_alloc.end()) return;         // not a slab pointer
    const size_t off = (size_t) ((char *) ptr - (char *) g_slab_base);
    const size_t sz = it->second;
    g_slab_alloc.erase(it);
    g_slab_free.push_back({ off, sz });
    std::sort(g_slab_free.begin(), g_slab_free.end());
    std::vector<std::pair<size_t, size_t>> merged;
    for (const auto & r : g_slab_free) {
        if (!merged.empty() && merged.back().first + merged.back().second == r.first) {
            merged.back().second += r.second;
        } else {
            merged.push_back(r);
        }
    }
    g_slab_free.swap(merged);
}

static void sb_mmid_reset() {
    std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(g_mmid_mutex);
    // Entries are slab sub-ranges (or, if the slab never initialised, plain
    // cudaMalloc'd blocks). Return each to whichever allocator produced it.
    for (const auto & entry : g_mmid_entries) {
        if (!entry.device) continue;
        if (entry.slab) {
            if (g_slab_base) sb_slab_free(entry.device);
        } else {
            cudaFree(entry.device);
        }
    }
    g_mmid_entries.clear();
    g_mmid_capacity = sb_mmid_capacity_bytes();
    g_mmid_bytes = 0;
    g_mmid_tick = 0;
    g_mmid_hits = 0;
    g_mmid_misses = 0;
    g_mmid_evictions = 0;
    g_mmid_bytes_fetched = 0;
    g_mp_reserved = 0;
    g_mmid_epoch = 0;
    g_mmid_calls = 0;
    g_mmid_tokens = 0;
    g_mmid_overflow = 0;
    g_mmid_verify_ok = 0;
    g_mmid_verify_fail = 0;
    // "1": every fetch AND every hit (small tests only: a long prefill would
    //      copy terabytes back); "fetch": every fetch (miss) plus every 64th
    //      hit, chosen by the hit counter, never by an outcome.
    const char * v = std::getenv("SABAH_LLAMA_VERIFY_BYTES");
    g_mmid_verify = v && (std::strcmp(v, "1") == 0 || std::strcmp(v, "fetch") == 0);
    g_mmid_verify_all_hits = v && std::strcmp(v, "1") == 0;
    g_mmid_verify_budget = UINT64_MAX;
    if (const char * lim = std::getenv("SABAH_LLAMA_VERIFY_LIMIT")) {
        g_mmid_verify_budget = (uint64_t) std::strtoull(lim, nullptr, 10);
    }
    g_mmid_empty_calls = 0;
    g_id_reuse = 0;
    g_id_group_max = 0;
    g_id_check_ok = 0;
    g_id_check_fail = 0;
    {
        const char * ic = std::getenv("SABAH_LLAMA_ID_CHECK");
        g_id_check = ic && (std::strcmp(ic, "1") == 0 || std::strcmp(ic, "on") == 0);
    }
    const char * k = std::getenv("SABAH_LLAMA_SELFCHECK");
    g_sc_k = k ? std::atoi(k) : 0;
    const char * tol = std::getenv("SABAH_LLAMA_SELFCHECK_TOL");
    g_sc_tol = tol ? std::atof(tol) : 2e-6;
    g_sc_ok = g_sc_fail = g_sc_multi_token = 0;
    g_sc_max = 0.0;
    for (auto & c : g_sc_slot) c = 0;
    g_sc_tensors.clear();
}

static void sb_mmid_evict_until(size_t needed, cudaStream_t compute_stream) {
    // No stream synchronisation belongs here, and saying why matters because the
    // obvious "drain before reuse" is what this used to do - twice per eviction,
    // which at roughly seven hundred evictions per token cost more than the
    // expert reads themselves.
    //
    // Every entry that can be evicted here was created by an EARLIER MUL_MAT_ID
    // call. Each call begins by downloading the router ids on the compute stream
    // and synchronising that stream, so by the time this runs:
    //   * the previous calls' kernels have completed, and
    //   * each of those kernels had already waited on the copy-stream event that
    //     ordered its own host-to-device expert transfers,
    // so both the kernel and the transfer that touched a victim are finished.
    // Entries created by the call in progress are never victims: their pointers
    // are already in that call's pointer table and its kernel has not run yet.
    // If nothing else can be evicted the tier temporarily exceeds its budget
    // instead of producing a dangling pointer.
    // The router-id reuse keeps up to SB_ID_GROUP_MAX calls of one layer from
    // draining the compute stream, so kernels from the last epochs can still be
    // reading their slots. Their entries are held back as victims for as many
    // epochs as that window is wide; older ones are provably finished.
    (void) compute_stream;
    while (g_mmid_bytes + needed > g_mmid_capacity) {
        auto victim = g_mmid_entries.end();
        for (auto it = g_mmid_entries.begin(); it != g_mmid_entries.end(); ++it) {
            if (it->epoch + SB_ID_GROUP_MAX > g_mmid_epoch) continue;
            if (victim == g_mmid_entries.end() || it->touch < victim->touch) victim = it;
        }
        if (victim == g_mmid_entries.end()) {
            ++g_mmid_overflow;
            return;
        }
        if (victim->slab) {
            if (g_slab_base) sb_slab_free(victim->device);
        } else {
            cudaFree(victim->device);
        }
        g_mmid_bytes -= victim->bytes;
        // Order in the table is irrelevant: recency is `touch`. Swapping the last
        // element in avoids shifting the tens of thousands of entries behind the
        // victim, which cost more than the disk read it made room for.
        *victim = g_mmid_entries.back();
        g_mmid_entries.pop_back();
        ++g_mmid_evictions;
    }
}

// Byte-exact check of a resident slot against the original GGUF range. The
// oracle is the mapped file, so every check pulls those pages into RAM - the
// budget bounds that, otherwise a long prefill would fill the machine with the
// pages the verifier itself asked for.
//
// A verification failure also says *where* the served bytes came from, because a
// bare "differs" is not actionable. The origin of every pending fetch is kept
// while verification is on, so the mismatch line can name the file and offset.
struct sb_fetch_origin {
    const void * dst;
    size_t       file;
    uint64_t     off;
    size_t       bytes;
};
static std::mutex                   g_origin_mutex;
static std::vector<sb_fetch_origin> g_origin;
// The last object a verification rejected, so the error message the host sees can
// name the file and offset instead of only "differs".
static size_t                       g_verify_last_file = (size_t) -1;
static uint64_t                     g_verify_last_off  = 0;

// Defined after the disk-file table; used only to name a file in a diagnostic.
static const char * sb_disk_file_path(size_t i, uint64_t * size_out);

static bool sb_mmid_verify(const void * device, const void * source, size_t bytes) {
    if (g_mmid_verify_budget == 0) return true;   // budget spent: assert nothing
    if (g_mmid_verify_budget != UINT64_MAX) --g_mmid_verify_budget;
    std::vector<uint8_t> back(bytes);
    if (cudaStreamSynchronize(g_copy) != cudaSuccess ||
        cudaMemcpy(back.data(), device, bytes, cudaMemcpyDeviceToHost) != cudaSuccess) {
        ++g_mmid_verify_fail;
        return false;
    }
    if (std::memcmp(back.data(), source, bytes) != 0) {
        ++g_mmid_verify_fail;
        // How they differ is the whole diagnosis: a mismatch at byte ~0 with the
        // rest matching is a placement error, a contiguous run at the end is a
        // short read (the staging buffer's stale tail), and mismatches scattered
        // through the whole object is the wrong object.
        const uint8_t * a = back.data();
        const uint8_t * b = (const uint8_t *) source;
        size_t nmis = 0, firsts[8], nf = 0, runs = 0, prev = (size_t) -1;
        for (size_t i = 0; i < bytes; ++i) {
            if (a[i] != b[i]) {
                ++nmis;
                if (prev == (size_t) -1 || i != prev + 1) ++runs;
                prev = i;
                if (nf < 8) firsts[nf++] = i;
            }
        }
        char pos[8 * 12 + 1];
        size_t po = 0;
        for (size_t i = 0; i < nf; ++i) {
            po += (size_t) snprintf(pos + po, sizeof(pos) - po, "%s%llu",
                                    i ? "," : "", (unsigned long long) firsts[i]);
        }
        const char * where = "unresolved";
        uint64_t fsize = 0;
        g_verify_last_file = (size_t) -1;
        g_verify_last_off  = 0;
        {
            std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(g_origin_mutex);
            for (const auto & o : g_origin) {
                if (o.dst == device) {
                    g_verify_last_file = o.file;
                    g_verify_last_off  = o.off;
                    std::fprintf(stderr,
                                 "SABAH_VERIFY_MISMATCH bytes=%llu differing=%llu runs=%llu first=%s\n"
                                 "  read %llu bytes at offset %llu of file #%llu (%s, size %llu),"
                                 " source=%p\n",
                                 (unsigned long long) bytes, (unsigned long long) nmis,
                                 (unsigned long long) runs, pos,
                                 (unsigned long long) o.bytes, (unsigned long long) o.off,
                                 (unsigned long long) o.file, sb_disk_file_path(o.file, &fsize),
                                 (unsigned long long) fsize, source);
                    std::fflush(stderr);
                    return false;
                }
            }
        }
        std::fprintf(stderr,
                     "SABAH_VERIFY_MISMATCH bytes=%llu differing=%llu runs=%llu first=%s (%s)\n",
                     (unsigned long long) bytes, (unsigned long long) nmis,
                     (unsigned long long) runs, pos, where);
        std::fflush(stderr);
        return false;
    }
    ++g_mmid_verify_ok;
    return true;
}

// A pending expert fetch: the slot is reserved but its bytes are not in it yet.
struct sb_fetch {
    const void * source_key;
    int          expert;
    const void * source;   // mapped bytes: fallback route and verification oracle
    size_t       bytes;
    void *       dst;      // device slot
};

// Residency memo for one call. Every lookup of a call asks the same table for
// the same tensor, so one pass answers all of them; testing each expert with its
// own scan made `slot` the second largest cost after the disk. The pass also
// stamps recency, so a memo hit needs no entry access at all - which keeps it
// correct even when an eviction shifts the table underneath it.
//
// One snapshot PER TENSOR the call looks at, not one per call. A call may also
// reserve the experts of the tensor that follows it in the same layer (see the
// merged fetch in sabah_rt_mul_mat_id_v2), and that is a different tensor with
// its own residency and its own expert geometry.
struct sb_memo_hit { int expert; void * device; };

struct sb_memo {
    const void *             source = nullptr;
    size_t                   bytes  = 0;
    bool                     valid  = false;   // scan complete: absent means "not resident"
    std::vector<sb_memo_hit> hits;
};

static constexpr size_t SB_MEMO_MAX = 3;
static thread_local sb_memo g_memos[SB_MEMO_MAX];
static thread_local size_t   g_memos_n = 0;   // number of live snapshots this call
static thread_local std::vector<int> g_memo_ids;

static sb_memo * sb_memo_find(const void * source_key, size_t bytes) {
    for (size_t i = 0; i < g_memos_n; ++i) {
        if (g_memos[i].source == source_key && g_memos[i].bytes == bytes) return &g_memos[i];
    }
    return nullptr;
}

// `count_hits` is false when the snapshot is taken for a tensor that this call
// did not itself ask for: those experts are counted where they are actually
// used, by the call that asks for them, so the tier's hit and miss totals stay
// what they always were - only the wave they arrive in changes.
static void sb_mmid_prime(const void * source_key, size_t bytes,
                          const uint8_t * ids_raw, size_t id_stride, size_t token_stride,
                          int n_ids, int n_tokens, bool count_hits) {
    sb_phase_timer _t(&g_t_prime);
    sb_memo * memo = sb_memo_find(source_key, bytes);
    if (memo == nullptr) {
        if (g_memos_n >= SB_MEMO_MAX) return;
        memo = &g_memos[g_memos_n++];
        memo->source = source_key;
        memo->bytes  = bytes;
    }
    memo->valid = false;
    memo->hits.clear();
    if (n_ids <= 0 || n_tokens <= 0) return;

    g_memo_ids.clear();
    for (int t = 0; t < n_tokens; ++t) {
        for (int i = 0; i < n_ids; ++i) {
            const int e = *(const int32_t *) (ids_raw + (size_t) t * token_stride
                                                       + (size_t) i * id_stride);
            bool seen = false;
            for (int v : g_memo_ids) { if (v == e) { seen = true; break; } }
            if (!seen) g_memo_ids.push_back(e);
        }
    }

    std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(g_mmid_mutex);
    for (auto & entry : g_mmid_entries) {
        if (entry.source_key != source_key || entry.bytes != bytes || !entry.filled) continue;
        for (int v : g_memo_ids) {
            if (v != entry.expert) continue;
            entry.touch = ++g_mmid_tick;
            entry.epoch = g_mmid_epoch;
            if (count_hits) ++g_mmid_hits;
            if (count_hits && g_mmid_verify &&
                (g_mmid_verify_all_hits || g_mmid_hits % g_mmid_verify_hit_every == 0)) {
                const uint8_t * src = (const uint8_t *) source_key;
                src += (size_t) entry.expert * bytes;
                if (!sb_mmid_verify(entry.device, src, bytes)) {
                    memo->valid = false;
                    return;
                }
            }
            memo->hits.push_back({ entry.expert, entry.device });
            break;
        }
    }
    memo->valid = true;
}

// Turn one non-resident expert into a reserved device slot: make room, take the
// space from the slab, and record the fetch that will fill it. The caller holds
// g_mmid_mutex (eviction looks at the table it is working on, and does not lock
// it itself).
static void * sb_mmid_reserve_locked(const void * source_key, int expert,
                                     const void * source, size_t bytes,
                                     std::vector<sb_fetch> & miss,
                                     cudaStream_t compute_stream) {
    ++g_mmid_misses;
    sb_mmid_evict_until(bytes, compute_stream);

    void * device = sb_slab_alloc(bytes);
    bool from_slab = device != nullptr;
    if (!device) {
        // The pool is smaller than this working set and nothing else can be
        // evicted. Documented behaviour is to EXCEED the budget for this fetch
        // (a plain device allocation) rather than fail the call: the slot is
        // still freed normally when it is later evicted, and the routed result
        // stays exact. Overflow counts these over-budget allocations.
        ++g_mmid_overflow;
        if (!ck(cudaMalloc(&device, bytes), "Sabah over-budget expert")) return nullptr;
    }

    g_mmid_entries.push_back({ source_key, expert, device, bytes, ++g_mmid_tick,
                               g_mmid_epoch, from_slab, false });
    g_mmid_bytes += bytes;
    g_mmid_bytes_fetched += bytes;
    miss.push_back({ source_key, expert, source, bytes, device });
    return device;
}

// Reserve (or find) the device slot for one expert. This is pure bookkeeping -
// no I/O is issued here - so a whole MUL_MAT_ID call can be scanned first and
// then fetched with a bounded number of concurrent unbuffered reads.
static void * sb_mmid_slot(const void * source_key, int expert,
                           const void * source, size_t bytes,
                           std::vector<sb_fetch> & miss,
                           cudaStream_t compute_stream) {
    sb_memo * memo = sb_memo_find(source_key, bytes);
    if (memo != nullptr && memo->valid) {
        // The snapshot is complete, so it answers the question outright: a
        // resident expert comes back straight from it, and an expert that is
        // not in it is not resident at all, which saves the table scan that the
        // miss path used to repeat for every single lookup.
        for (const auto & h : memo->hits) {
            if (h.expert == expert) return h.device;
        }
        sb_phase_timer _t(&g_t_slot);
        std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(g_mmid_mutex);
        if (g_mmid_capacity == 0) {
            g_mmid_capacity = sb_mmid_capacity_bytes();
        }
        void * device = sb_mmid_reserve_locked(source_key, expert, source, bytes,
                                               miss, compute_stream);
        if (device != nullptr) memo->hits.push_back({ expert, device });
        return device;
    }

    sb_phase_timer _t(&g_t_slot);
    std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(g_mmid_mutex);
    if (g_mmid_capacity == 0) {
        g_mmid_capacity = sb_mmid_capacity_bytes();
    }

    for (auto & entry : g_mmid_entries) {
        // `filled` is what makes a slot a hit. An entry created earlier in this
        // same call is already reserved for us, so it is returned as well.
        if (entry.source_key == source_key && entry.expert == expert && entry.bytes == bytes &&
            (entry.filled || entry.epoch == g_mmid_epoch)) {
            entry.touch = ++g_mmid_tick;
            entry.epoch = g_mmid_epoch;
            ++g_mmid_hits;
            if (entry.filled && g_mmid_verify &&
                (g_mmid_verify_all_hits || g_mmid_hits % g_mmid_verify_hit_every == 0) &&
                !sb_mmid_verify(entry.device, source, bytes)) {
                snprintf(g_err, sizeof(g_err),
                         "Sabah resident expert %d differs from its GGUF bytes (hit): the slot was "
                         "delivered earlier in this run (see SABAH_VERIFY_MISMATCH; a stale OS page "
                         "cache for the model file is the usual cause, not the read)", expert);
                return nullptr;
            }
            return entry.device;
        }
    }

    return sb_mmid_reserve_locked(source_key, expert, source, bytes, miss, compute_stream);
}

static bool ck(cudaError_t e, const char * what) {
    if (e == cudaSuccess) return true;
    snprintf(g_err, sizeof(g_err), "%s: %s", what, cudaGetErrorString(e));
    return false;
}

// ===========================================================================
// Direct expert streaming:  file -> pinned staging -> VRAM slot
// ===========================================================================
//
// The expert bank is 50.29 GB and this machine has 16 GB of RAM. Reading those
// bytes through llama.cpp's mmap would push every fetched entity through the
// Windows file cache, so the box would spend its RAM caching expert bytes that
// a multi-gigabyte VRAM tier is going to ask for again only rarely - and that
// is exactly what makes it thrash.
//
// Instead the bytes are read with FILE_FLAG_NO_BUFFERING straight from the GGUF
// file into a pinned, sector-aligned staging block and copied on to their device
// slot. The resident host footprint is a fixed few tens of megabytes no matter
// how large the model is, and the OS file cache is never touched.
//
// The runtime is a drop-in adapter, so it is not told which file backs a given
// host pointer. Windows has no inverse-mapping API, but a mapped view is a
// single MEM_MAPPED allocation: VirtualQuery reports its base and
// GetMappedFileName names the file. llama.cpp maps whole files from offset 0,
// which the "GGUF" magic at the view base confirms before anything is trusted.
// ===========================================================================

static bool sb_direct_io_enabled() {
    const char * v = std::getenv("SABAH_LLAMA_DIRECT_IO");
    return !(v && (std::strcmp(v, "0") == 0 || std::strcmp(v, "off") == 0));
}

static size_t sb_io_depth() {
    const char * v = std::getenv("SABAH_LLAMA_IO_DEPTH");
    long n = v ? std::atol(v) : 8;
    if (n < 1)  n = 1;
    if (n > 32) n = 32;
    return (size_t) n;
}

// Unbuffered I/O requires the offset, the length and the buffer to share an
// alignment that is a multiple of the volume sector size. 4096 satisfies both
// 512e and 4Kn volumes.
static const size_t SB_IO_ALIGN = 4096;

// Read lanes: synchronous readers, one file object each. The count is a thread
// count - see the read pool for the measurement that forced it.
static const int SB_IO_LANES = 8;

struct sb_disk_file {
#if defined(_WIN32)
    HANDLE h = INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE;   // overlapped: the one-off and prefetch reads
    // One SYNCHRONOUS handle per read lane. See the read pool below for why the
    // lanes are threads with their own file object rather than requests on one.
    HANDLE lane[SB_IO_LANES];
    int    lanes = 0;
#else
    int    fd = -1;
#endif
    uint64_t    size = 0;
    std::string path;
};

static std::mutex                    g_disk_mutex;
static std::vector<sb_disk_file>     g_disk_files;
static std::map<std::string, size_t> g_disk_paths;

// Forward-declared beside sb_mmid_verify, which needs it in a diagnostic.
static const char * sb_disk_file_path(size_t i, uint64_t * size_out) {
    if (size_out) *size_out = 0;
    if (i >= g_disk_files.size()) return "file out of range";
    if (size_out) *size_out = g_disk_files[i].size;
    return g_disk_files[i].path.c_str();
}

#if defined(_WIN32)
// "\Device\HarddiskVolume3\Strata-data\x.gguf" -> "$DRIVE\Strata-data\x.gguf"
static bool sb_device_path_to_dos(const std::string & dev, std::string & out) {
    char drives[1024];
    const DWORD n = GetLogicalDriveStringsA((DWORD) sizeof(drives) - 1, drives);
    if (n == 0 || n >= sizeof(drives)) return false;
    for (const char * d = drives; d < drives + n && *d; d += std::strlen(d) + 1) {
        char target[1024];
        char drive[4];
        snprintf(drive, sizeof(drive), "%c:", d[0]);   // QueryDosDevice wants "C:"
        if (!QueryDosDeviceA(drive, target, sizeof(target))) continue;
        const size_t tl = std::strlen(target);
        if (dev.size() >= tl && dev.compare(0, tl, target) == 0) {
            out = d;                                        // "$DRIVE\"
            if (!out.empty() && out.back() == '\\') out.pop_back();
            out += dev.substr(tl);
            return true;
        }
    }
    return false;
}
#endif

// Must be called with g_disk_mutex held. Returns the file index or (size_t)-1.
static size_t sb_disk_open_locked(const std::string & path) {
    auto it = g_disk_paths.find(path);
    if (it != g_disk_paths.end()) return it->second;

    sb_disk_file f;
    f.path = path;
#if defined(_WIN32)
    f.h = CreateFileA(path.c_str(), GENERIC_READ, FILE_SHARE_READ | FILE_SHARE_WRITE,
                      nullptr, OPEN_EXISTING,
                      FILE_FLAG_NO_BUFFERING | FILE_FLAG_OVERLAPPED, nullptr);
    if (f.h == INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE) return (size_t) -1;
    for (auto & l : f.lane) l = INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE;
    f.lanes = 0;
    // The read lanes, opened here so that a shard registered later still has
    // them: synchronous handle (no FILE_FLAG_OVERLAPPED), one per lane.
    for (int i = 0; i < SB_IO_LANES; ++i) {
        HANDLE lane = CreateFileA(path.c_str(), GENERIC_READ,
                                  FILE_SHARE_READ | FILE_SHARE_WRITE, nullptr,
                                  OPEN_EXISTING, FILE_FLAG_NO_BUFFERING, nullptr);
        if (lane == INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE) break;
        f.lane[f.lanes++] = lane;
    }
    LARGE_INTEGER li;
    if (!GetFileSizeEx(f.h, &li)) { CloseHandle(f.h); return (size_t) -1; }
    f.size = (uint64_t) li.QuadPart;
#else
    f.fd = ::open(path.c_str(), O_RDONLY | O_DIRECT);
    if (f.fd < 0) return (size_t) -1;
    struct stat st;
    if (fstat(f.fd, &st) != 0) { ::close(f.fd); return (size_t) -1; }
    f.size = (uint64_t) st.st_size;
#endif
    g_disk_paths[path] = g_disk_files.size();
    g_disk_files.push_back(f);
    return g_disk_files.size() - 1;
}

// A host pointer that lives in a memory-mapped file -> which file and where.
struct sb_host_map {
    const uint8_t * base;
    uint64_t        offset;   // file offset of `base`
    uint64_t        size;     // covered range, so two files cannot be confused
    size_t          file;
};

static std::vector<sb_host_map> g_host_maps;

// Resolve a mapped host pointer to (file, byte offset in that file).
// `trace`, when given, receives the reason for failure - the runtime must be
// able to explain why it fell back to the mapped bytes.
static bool sb_resolve(const void * source, size_t & file_idx, uint64_t & file_off,
                       std::string * trace = nullptr) {
#if defined(_WIN32)
    const uint8_t * p = (const uint8_t *) source;

    {
        std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(g_disk_mutex);
        for (const auto & m : g_host_maps) {
            if (p >= m.base && p < m.base + m.size) {
                file_idx = m.file;
                file_off = (uint64_t) (p - m.base) + m.offset;
                return true;
            }
        }
    }

    MEMORY_BASIC_INFORMATION mbi;
    if (VirtualQuery(source, &mbi, sizeof(mbi)) == 0) {
        if (trace) *trace = "VirtualQuery failed";
        return false;
    }

    // The state of the region the pointer sits in is NOT what identifies the
    // mapping. llama.cpp unmaps fragments of a view once their bytes have been
    // copied elsewhere, so a tensor can legitimately point into a MEM_RESERVE
    // hole inside a MEM_MAPPED allocation. What identifies the mapping is the
    // allocation base, which is the view base, which carries the GGUF magic
    // because llama.cpp maps whole files from offset 0.
    const uint8_t * base = (const uint8_t *) mbi.AllocationBase;
    if (base == nullptr || base > p) {
        if (trace) *trace = "allocation base is not below the pointer";
        return false;
    }
    if (std::memcmp(base, "GGUF", 4) != 0) {
        char b[192];
        snprintf(b, sizeof(b),
                 "no GGUF magic at allocation base (Type=%lu State=%lu first bytes %02x %02x %02x %02x)",
                 (unsigned long) mbi.Type, (unsigned long) mbi.State,
                 base[0], base[1], base[2], base[3]);
        if (trace) *trace = b;
        return false;
    }

    char devname[4096];
    if (GetMappedFileNameA(GetCurrentProcess(), (LPVOID) base, devname,
                           (DWORD) sizeof(devname)) == 0) {
        if (trace) {
            char b[160];
            snprintf(b, sizeof(b), "GetMappedFileName failed (err %lu)", (unsigned long) GetLastError());
            *trace = b;
        }
        return false;
    }
    std::string path;
    if (!sb_device_path_to_dos(devname, path)) {
        if (trace) *trace = std::string("cannot map device path to a drive: ") + devname;
        return false;
    }

    std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(g_disk_mutex);
    const size_t fi = sb_disk_open_locked(path);
    if (fi == (size_t) -1) {
        if (trace) {
            char b[160];
            snprintf(b, sizeof(b), "cannot open unbuffered: %s (err %lu)",
                     path.c_str(), (unsigned long) GetLastError());
            *trace = b;
        }
        return false;
    }
    g_host_maps.push_back({ base, 0, g_disk_files[fi].size, fi });
    file_idx = fi;
    file_off = (uint64_t) (p - base);
    return true;
#else
    (void) source; (void) file_idx; (void) file_off;
    if (trace) *trace = "direct I/O is Windows only in this build";
    return false;
#endif
}

// --------------------------- pinned staging pool ---------------------------

struct sb_io_slot {
    void * staging = nullptr;
    size_t cap     = 0;
#if defined(_WIN32)
    HANDLE ev      = nullptr;
#endif
};

static std::vector<sb_io_slot> g_io_slots;
static std::mutex              g_io_slots_mutex;

// One event per staging parity, recorded on the copy stream after a wave's
// host-to-device transfers so the following reuse of those buffers can wait for
// them. Two entries are enough for double buffering.
static cudaEvent_t g_io_wave_ev[2] = { nullptr, nullptr };

static sb_io_slot * sb_io_slot_at(size_t i, size_t need) {
    std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(g_io_slots_mutex);
    while (g_io_slots.size() <= i) {
        sb_io_slot s;
#if defined(_WIN32)
        s.ev = CreateEventA(nullptr, TRUE, FALSE, nullptr);
        if (s.ev == nullptr) return nullptr;
#endif
        g_io_slots.push_back(s);
    }
    sb_io_slot & s = g_io_slots[i];
    if (s.cap < need) {
        if (s.staging) { cudaFreeHost(s.staging); s.staging = nullptr; }
        void * p = nullptr;
        if (!ck(cudaHostAlloc(&p, need, cudaHostAllocDefault), "sabah io staging")) return nullptr;
        s.staging = p;
        s.cap = need;
    }
    return &s;
}

static void sb_io_pool_destroy() {
    std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(g_io_slots_mutex);
    for (auto & s : g_io_slots) {
        if (s.staging) { cudaFreeHost(s.staging); s.staging = nullptr; }
#if defined(_WIN32)
